{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CfkHQcn6WTNyEVQVY518i","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I've parallelized the benchmark with MPI, OpenMP and CUDA. On this machine (4× RTX 3090), 1e8 options take **55 ms with 1 MPI rank, against 9.1 s for the original serial code (~165×)**. Running more ranks on this single node is slower, though: **~124 ms with 2 ranks and ~210–230 ms with 4**. That's because every rank has to send its results to rank 0, and that transfer limits the speed here.\n\nOnly `black-scholes/black_scholes.cpp` and `black-scholes/CMakeLists.txt` were changed. The executable is still `black_scholes`.\n\n**How it works**\n- **CUDA:** one GPU kernel builds each option exactly as `generateOptions` did and prices it on the spot, using the same Black-Scholes math. No input array is created or copied to the GPU. I left fast-math off to keep `erf`/`exp`/`log` accurate.\n- **MPI:** each rank takes a contiguous block of options. Ranks are matched to the GPUs on their own node. Results are sent to rank 0 in 32 MB chunks while the GPU works on the next chunk. Rank 0 has all receives posted in advance and keeps them moving while its own GPUs compute. Only rank 0 prints, and it shares the validation exit code with the other ranks.\n- **OpenMP:** if a rank has more than one GPU (for example one rank per node), it runs one thread per GPU. Each thread double-buffers with two CUDA streams. If the MPI library doesn't support full thread safety, the sends are made from the main thread after the GPU work instead.\n- **Fixed setup costs** (GPU init, memory allocation, a warm-up kernel) happen before the timer starts. The timed section covers pricing plus collecting all results on rank 0, so it matches the original's \"all results available\" meaning.\n- **CMake** compiles the `.cpp` with nvcc (`LANGUAGE CUDA`, C++20, GPU architecture `native` unless you set one) and links MPI, OpenMP and the CUDA runtime. No new external dependencies.\n\n**Correctness**\n- Output is identical across 1, 2 and 4 ranks: same sum, samples and hash. Validation passes, and the per-option validation lines match the original exactly.\n- The hash does **not** match the serial build bit-for-bit. GPU `erf`/`exp`/`log` differ from glibc in the last bits, and gcc also fuses multiply-adds differently. The sum agrees with the serial run to about 2e-16 relative. The largest difference I saw was about 3e-13 relative on one large call price, where two big numbers nearly cancel. An exact match would mean porting glibc's math routines to the GPU.\n- Edge cases work: `-n 0`, fewer options than ranks (some ranks get none), `-h`, and an unknown option (exits with code 1).\n\n**Scaling**\n\n| Ranks (1 node, 4 GPUs) | Time for 1e8 options |\n|---|---|\n| 1 (4 OpenMP threads, one per GPU) | 55 ms |\n| 2 | ~124 ms |\n| 4 | ~210–230 ms |\n\nWith more than one rank on a node, results travel to rank 0 through Open MPI's shared memory at roughly 3.5 GB/s. That transfer can't be avoided, because rank 0 needs the full array to print or validate results. For the best performance, launch **one MPI rank per node** so OpenMP drives all of that node's GPUs, and add nodes for more scale. I tried different chunk sizes, the transfer settings Open MPI offers here, and unpinned memory on rank 0; the current setup was the fastest."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":426,"cache_read_input_tokens":47523,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":426},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ccf29050-770c-484b-909c-40192e9fabef","uuid":"10f3312d-9f16-4c82-9ea9-44b52b6d6569","timestamp":"2026-10-06T03:36:20.805Z","request_id":"req_011CfkHQcXiCEPNT5rPGwtHw"}
